ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-22-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u192	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. It's an acctorial introduction first. However, we will remember the resolution well because we adopted it on the committee on the eve of the reform day and at the plenary session on the Maistre Day. Holidays can be taken and celebrated either by fun or by work. For me, dealing with a resolution in such circumstances is particularly valuable. In the Parliamentary Group of Social Democrats, we will support the draft resolution on the National Programme for Culture 2014-2017. It is high time to redefine the country's strategic direction in this area after almost two years of declaration eclipse. After two resolutions – the first presented by Minister Andreja Richter in 2004 and the second in 2008 by Minister Vasko Simoniti – this is the third document produced under the 2002 Law on the Realisation of Public Interest in Culture. Let us recall that the first attempt to create a development document on culture dates back to 2000, when the debate on the proposal by Minister Rudi Sheliga in the National Assembly was preceded by an election. In the Group of Social Democrats, we understand the Resolution on the National Programme for Culture as a far-reaching challenge of cultural policy to ensure public interest in this area. At the declaration level, it covers substantive arguments for the preparation of the annual budget, directs the work of the competent minister and the Government, sets priorities for investment in public cultural infrastructure, which are also based on the Cultural Tolar Act, and last but not least offers an opportunity to discuss annually the state of this constitutional element of national identity with us. The proposed resolution was accompanied by high expectations from the public, also encouraged by the Minister, as its draft in June this year was presented as a new model of cultural policy. The question is whether the document achieves this definition. The resolution comes at a time of a limited public financial framework, with limited opportunities to deal effectively with the situation of the economic and social crisis. Notwithstanding those circumstances, we are particularly pleased with the way in which the proposal was prepared, which allowed for broad public participation. This was not least recognised by the National Council for Culture and considered that a number of proposals were included in the proposal by the professional public. In our opinion, this direction of the Ministry creates the conditions for continued dialogue, which we missed at the time of the absence of the Ministry of Culture. The draft resolution on the national programme for culture 2014-2017 at the principal level adequately reflects the state of cultural space in Slovenia. It responds mainly to the proposals offered in the past by individual state associations or the public concerned, but nevertheless presents some innovative approaches. The document thus offers a very solid section of the needs and demands of culture in the coming period. In particular, we welcome the diplomatic approach to introducing cultural change, which places great emphasis on the involvement of the public concerned. We are convinced that the application of measures in practice could also bring about significant progress in understanding the importance of the presence of culture. Among the works that we want to highlight is a chapter dedicated to the labour market in culture and self-employed people. At the same time, we find it particularly important to emphasise the priority support given to young people who suffer the worst consequences of the labour market during the crisis, and the behaviour of the previous government has further deepened their difficulties. Special attention in the Group of Social Democrats is paid to the culture of lovers. On this occasion, we draw attention to the efforts of the Association of Cultural Society after a clearer legal arrangement and express our expectation that the competent ministry will examine and take into account the proposals made in the preparation of the sectoral legislation. In the Group of Social Democrats, we will support the motion for a resolution, and for the reasons set out below, we deny support for the proposed amendments that are not of the coalition. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-22-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u243	Thank you. A representative of the Government, Dr. Aleš Črnič, has the floor.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-22-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u244	Thank you very much. Given that Mr Jerovšek wanted an immediate direct answer, we are happy to answer this question, given that you addressed it directly to us. The title of this chapter includes the word "minority", so that this "minority" does not mean a constitutionally recognised national minority. This "minority" also means minorities in different respects, not only in national terms. This chapter includes three measures aimed at ethnic minorities, as well as other minorities, persons with disabilities, for example. It is therefore impossible, in a substantive sense, to change the title of this chapter to the national community, since the remaining minority types should then be excluded from this chapter. So it's not about introducing real minorities, new constitutional minorities through the side door, or other conscious purposes, but it's about the content structure of this chapter that is such that it talks about different types of minorities, and it's not just about national minorities. However, as regards the second amendment, this complements a measure already aimed at ethnic communities or groups alone within this broader objective and thus does not contribute in a substantive sense. The matter is already covered in this chapter. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-22-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u257	Thank you. Dr. Aleš Crnitch, please.
ParlaMint-SI_2013-11-22-SDZ6-Redna-19.ana.u258	Thank you very much. Just once more to avoid misunderstandings. Of course, the topics that Mr Jerovšek is creating are very important topics that this country is facing and will have to face and will have to resolve. But these topics are not the subject of a national cultural programme. The Ministry of Culture does not interfere with the status issues of minorities when we talk about national minorities defined by the legislator either by constitution or by declaration or by inter-State agreements. Mr Jerovšek, you have only found that in the very text of this chapter, as you say, we use the right terms, in short, as you see fit, but in those parts of this chapter when we talk about national minorities, we also know other types of minorities. We Slovenians are sometimes a minority in our country. A religious minority is an expression we know, a sexual minority is an expression we know. And this chapter speaks of different types of minorities, including national minorities. And where we are talking about national minorities, we are using expressions that you yourself, Mr Jerovšek, have recognized as correct or, as you say, correct. So, after a thorough review, after consulting lawyers, we are convinced that such a statement in this chapter cannot possibly be preconceived for questions which are otherwise entirely on the ground.
